# The 48-AsM fleet review of Drone-2525 at r.147 → r.148 (operator 2026-09-23: "target approve and fire keeps resetting to 50 m left target; fix · test all work with 48 AsM fleet and MoT")

Method: 12 lenses (the twelve AsM names, one testing super-power each) × (reviewer A on the SERVED r.147 source · reviewer B playing the served r.147 adversarially through the harnesses and its own headless probes · one synthesis per lens over A + B) + a thirteenth B seat (Athena-2) that replayed the operator's complaint touch-only on r.146 and r.147 + 12 MoT coordinators over cross-cutting questions = 48 read-only agents at the configured model and effort (never overridden). Each report is written verbatim under the session scratchpad (`fleet147/<LENS>-<ROLE>.md`) and reproduced here in full below the tables; every claim cites the served source (r.147 sha256 2476f68a…). The fold that answered the fleet is **r.148** (`docs/drone-2525/operator-deck/patches/r147_to_r148.py`, register §r.148).

## The convergent finding, and what r.148 folded

Every lens that touched the range reached the same cause: r.147's rule (LOCK is the target nearest the bullseye on the picture) ran only on an EMPTY board. `lockOn` kept the marked target — mine, the other seat's, or the AI's — anywhere inside a 35° cone BEFORE the pip rule, and r.147's own gate cleared the board before it looked. The deck parks the head on a 50 m plate; the first TARGET marks it; from then on every TARGET re-marked that plate and swung the head back, APPROVE reddened it under a ring on another plate, FIRE said MISS. With the AI member on, it marked the nearest plate in metres the moment a box cleared and swung the seated head onto it. Athena-2 and Athena-B reproduced the operator's sentence verbatim on r.147 in both orientations. r.148 folds the class as one rule with its members — see `REVISIONS.md` §r.148 for the eight members, the nine new QA rows (160/161 in each orientation) and the fleet's owed list carried to r.149.

## The twelve syntheses — one package per lens over A + B, verbatim

### Aset

LENS: Aset · SYNTHESIS · deck r.147 (play.html sha256 2476f68a…; A read the source, B played it; every disagreement re-read against play.html)

STATE AT r.147
HELD
- Clean board, pip within 48 px: LOCK line (1824), T1/key 1 (2197→1929, 2210), TARGET (4393), toast, caption (1849), board (2402) all name the 150 R; APPROVE reds, FIRE lands. Gates LOCK_IS_NEAREST_TO_THE_PIP (3937), TARGET_MARKS_THE_PIP (3996), CAP_LOCK_IS_THE_PIP; A+B agree, both orientations.
- Off the picture never a candidate (1929); sky → "NO TARGET UNDER THE BULLSEYE" for key 1 and button alike (A P6, B probe B lane 7).
- Two seats agree on lock, slots, strip, score, lastShot and replay hash at every step (A team e2e H==J; B a8abb2a5). Joiner FIRE / host self-APPROVE refused with the right sentence.
- B's "key 1 refuses after the hit" is the harness pressing inside RETURN_S while the plate lies down — rule as declared, not a defect. B's "strip AMBER while desig red" is a ≤2-rAF read race, not human-visible.
NOT HELD
1. THE OPERATOR'S SYMPTOM SURVIVES r.147 (A only; B missed it because B's probe C ran with desig=null). lockOn 1934-1937 returns state.desig whenever it is inside a 35° cone (dot>0.82) with NO px reach — the r.147 reach (1929-1930) is applied only when no amber exists. TARGET button (4393) and voice (3608) call lockOn(); key 1 (2210) calls slots()/pipRank instead. Repro A-P1: mark the 50 L, put the pip 20 px beside the 150 R → LOCK line "C-50L-L01", TARGET re-marks the 50 L and swings the head 6.77°, APPROVE+FIRE hits the 50 L; key 1 marks the 150 R into slot 2. Gate 3996 executes `state.tgtSlot={}; state.desig=null` BEFORE targetN(1), so it never sees the branch. Settled by line 1936.
2. Keys 2/3 ignore the reach (B, A agrees): 2210 guards `n===1` only. Repro B lane 7 pan −6.5: key 1/TARGET refuse, key 2 marks C-150R 141 px off and swings 4.4°, APPROVE→RED, FIRE hits. Help line 400 presents 1/2/3 as equals.
3. Voice "TARGET N" ≠ key N (A): 3608 designate(lockOn()) into slot N (T1's target wearing a T2 label) vs 3581 targetN(2) = second-nearest. Same slot number, two plates. controls-schema:102 never drives the grammar.
4. Two "under the pip" definitions + two reach thresholds (A, merged): lockOn rect-pass 1943 (nearest in METRES among overlapping outlines) vs slots() 2197 (centre-nearest px) → 79/796 pip positions where LOCK/TARGET = 100 L but T1/key 1 = 150 L; reticleNear 1923-1926 reds the bullseye at outline+8/28 px while lockOn locks at 48 px → LOCK text drawn under a WHITE bullseye (off=20).
5. Phase and name split across surfaces (A #5+#6, B #2+#4+#5, merged): caption phase from state.desig (1849) vs box (1816) and board (2402) from tgtSlot → after APPROVE slot 1 the T2 caption drops AMBER while its box/board stay amber; id "C-150R-L01" on LOCK/board, "150 M RIGHT" on toasts, "150M E" on captions; two-seat APPROVE toast reads state.desig.sameDevice (2289, where the MARK came from) so the joiner who just approved reads "APPROVED BY THE OTHER SEAT" and the host reads raw "RED · PEER HI-2 · C-50-L22"; at 390×844 #board (top:44, :37) prints over #playHud (top:42, :28); boot toast "QA 149/150" vs ops 151/152.
6. AI seat (B): asmTick (2495) re-marks in the same frame as the HIT, so "HIT" is never read on the AI seat; asm-play scores 0 hits from the toast while the strip says HIT 4. Slot number is per device on peerDesig 2242 (A #7): FIRE_SLOT_MISMATCH refuses by a number the two seats do not share.

GRADE at r.147: C — one rule declared, four callers (button/voice via lockOn's held amber, key 1 via pipRank, keys 2/3 via nothing), and the gate that would have caught the operator's complaint clears the board before it looks.

THE ONE SENTENCE: r.147 fixed TARGET only for an empty board — the moment any amber exists, lockOn (1934-1937) hands the button back the old mark from anywhere in a 35° cone, which is exactly "approve and fire keeps resetting to 50 m left", and gate 3996 nulls state.desig before testing so it cannot fail.

OWED r.148
1. Hysteresis obeys the reach: lockOn keeps state.desig only while its projection is ≤ LOCK_REACH_PX from the pip (else falls through to the pool), slots() applies the same hold so T1 == LOCK, targetN refuses ANY n with px > LOCK_REACH_PX. Gate AMBER_HELD_ONLY_UNDER_THE_PIP: mark the 50 L, pip 20 px beside the 150 R WITHOUT clearing desig → LOCK/TARGET/key 1/key 2/voice all = 150 R, head < 1.5°; plus KEYS_2_3_OBEY_THE_REACH beside 3937 and a range-2525 row.
2. One "under the pip", one reach: lockOn's rect-pass ranks by px like pipRank; reticleNear returns lockOn() unfiltered. Gate LOCK_EQUALS_T1_EVERYWHERE (P5 scan = 0 mismatches) + RETICLE_RED_IFF_LOCK (bullseye red ⇔ LOCK line drawn).
3. One phase, one name, one sentence per seat: captions/board/box read tgtSlot[n].phase and print plateWord(id); voice "TARGET N" calls targetN(N); APPROVE toast names the approver from `actor`, not the mark's origin; HIT toast survives asmTick one exposure and asm-play scores from state.rangeHit; #board off the strip at 390 px. Gates SURFACES_SAY_ONE_THING (two ambers, approve slot 1 → identical per slot), APPROVE_NAMES_THE_APPROVER (team e2e, both phones), AI_SEAT_SHOWS_THE_HIT, BOARD_NEVER_OVER_THE_STRIP (render-smoke), controls-schema:102 driving r.onresult.

HARDWARE-GATED: a thumb holding the pip inside 28 px of an 11 px plate on a 390 px phone (the band where class 4 bites); a real recognizer emitting "target two" as one commit; two handsets over real WebRTC/NAT proving the T-number and the APPROVE sentence the joiner reads; head-stick drift while a plate returns; 30 Hz bullseye colour vs LOCK text on a real GPU. Headless Chromium stands in for none.

### Asar

LENS: Asar · SYNTHESIS — OUTCOME TRUTH · deck r.147 (play.html sha256 2476f68a…, re-verified) · sources: Asar-A (source read, P1–P15), Asar-B (adversarial solo/AI/team, 39+19 steps); every disagreement re-read in play.html at the cited line.

STATE AT r.147
HELD
- Every round that leaves the barrel is one hashed row and the strip equals the record: HIT/MISS via netEvent :2328, rangeHit/rangeMiss on the same branch :2326, hudScore :2303 reads them; LAPSE rows per unengaged window; RELOAD|MANUAL :762; 42 HIT rows = 42 hits across three tables (B). Gate: HUD_SCORE_WRAPS + REDUCER_HIT_AND_LAPSE_DOWN_THE_PLATE.
- Every FIRE refusal is a REJECT/HOLD row: ROUND_NOT_OPEN :2346, NOT_THE_RED_TARGET :2349, NOT_THE_MARKERS_SEAT :2350, TARGET_GONE :2352, AMBER_NO_APPROVE/NO_RED_BOX :2353, WRONG_LANE :2354, EMPTY_MAGAZINE :2364 (no round spent). Fifth magazine REJECT NO_MAGAZINE :768. Gate: RELOAD_ON_RECORD + fleet REJECT rows = authFailures.
- Peer path is honest: decide() :2219 sends HOLD/REJECT/RESET/RELOAD to every peer; hash equal on both phones at all 19 team steps (B, 21ee5618). RESET button and mode select write RESET|RANGE via rangeResetOnRecord :4556 (double-ask is an ask, not a row). Gate: RESET_IS_A_ROW.
- T-box survives the head nudge and DESIGNATED names its marker (by:SID / ASM@) :1918. Gate: TARGET_MARKS_THE_PIP :3996.
NOT HELD (classes, duplicates merged)
1. A FIRE with no box is booked against a target nobody marked. fireN :2347 `s = state.desig || slots().find(n)`; slots() :2198 = pipRank top-3 with NO LOCK_REACH_PX filter; :2353 `decide('REJECT', s&&s.id||'NONE', NO_RED_BOX)`. Repro (B solo 04, team 09–10): TARGET refused "NO TARGET UNDER THE BULLSEYE", FIRE → screen "NO RED BOX", record REJECT|C-150R-L08 / C-100R-L08, replicated to the other phone and hashed. A missed it; B verified; settled NOT HELD. This is the record manufacturing the operator's complaint: it names the 50/100/150 plate he never marked.
2. Refusals at MARK and APPROVE time are not rows, while the trigger's and the peer's are (A-1 + B-3 merged). TARGET button on nothing: toast :4393. Key 1 beyond reach: log('TGT','none') to state.replay :2210 — outside replayHash (:2090 reads state.events). Voice: toast :3610. designate() refusals (round not open :1900, wrong lane :1902, target down :1903): toast only; peerDesig writes HOLD/REJECT for the same cases :2233-2234. APPROVE with no amber: toast :2268. ALL DOWN: noLockMsg toast. Repro: aim at the sky, press TARGET; replayHash unchanged; fleet: 11/12 qual40 engagements "refused" on screen, 0 in the hash.
3. The TARGET button moves the head on a mark the record says never happened: :4393 aimUnitAt runs after designate() unconditionally (A P3, room WAITING: head 0.63°, no row). B silent; verified NOT HELD.
4. Silent refusals on the shot path (A-3 + B-2 merged). QUAL SPENT: applyHit :2319 returns REFUSED, no row (B steps 24–27: three "ONE ROUND PER TARGET", Δevents 0). Worse: designate() has no _eng check :1899-1903, so key 1 re-marks the engaged plate → DESIGNATED, APPROVE, then nothing — an engagement the record leaves open. RELOAD when full: toast :767, no row.
5. A QUAL shot at a standing plate outside the engagement is a MISS row no tally sees: :2318 writes MISS|MISS DOWN and returns before qualRecordShot and rangeMiss; fireN :2377 still magSpend()s (result 'MISS' ≠ 'REFUSED'). A P6: rows [MISS], qualR 0, roundSpent 1. B silent; verified.
6. The strip zeroes without a RESET row on two user paths: modePick :4639 → goRange :4607 → rangeReset :800 (:4625), and lanePick :4634 → seatLane → goRange. A P11: "HIT 3 · MISS 3" → 0/0, rows []. Only rangeResetOnRecord :4556 writes RESET. B silent; verified.
7. AI seat: the HIT sentence is overwritten before it can be read. On a hit desig=null :2374; asmTick :2506 designates the next tick when !state.desig and toast() :2126 has no dwell guard, so "HIT · IN THE AIMING CIRCLE" lives one frame (B: 43–76 ms, 6/6). Record HIT 6, screen never showed one. A silent; code-verified.

GRADE at r.147: C+ — every round fired is on the record and both phones agree on it, but one hashed REJECT row names a target nobody marked and travels to the peer, and the mark's own refusals — r.147's headline sentence — leave no row at all. (A's B is withdrawn: A did not fire with an empty box.)

THE ONE SENTENCE: The record tells the truth about every shot and lies about every refusal — and when you fire with no box it writes the nearest plate's name as if you had chosen it, which is exactly the "50 m left" you keep seeing.

OWED r.148 (ordered by the operator's complaint)
1. A FIRE with no red box is booked against NONE (or the last mark that lapsed), never slots()[T1]: fireN :2347/:2353. Gate: REJECT_NEVER_NAMES_AN_UNMARKED_TARGET — pip on grass, TARGET refused, FIRE → row.id==='NONE' on both phones.
2. Every reset of the box or the head is a row that names its cause: goRange() takes a why and writes RESET|RANGE (SCENE/LANE) or does not zero the strip (:4625, :4634, :4639); the TARGET button aims only when designate() returned a mark (:4393); MARK/APPROVE refusals write decide('HOLD'|'REJECT', id|'NONE', {reason: NO_TARGET_UNDER_PIP|ROUND_NOT_OPEN|WRONG_LANE|TARGET_DOWN|NO_AMBER|ALL_DOWN, via: BUTTON|KEY|VOICE}) as peerDesig does. Gates: STRIP_ZERO_HAS_A_RESET_ROW + TARGET_REFUSED_HEAD_STAYS + REFUSAL_IS_A_ROW (every toast beginning NO / ALL DOWN ⇒ Δevents 1, key 1 == button == voice).
3. The shot path has no silent branch: SPENT → REJECT ONE_ROUND_PER_TARGET :2319 and designate() refuses an _eng plate with HOLD TARGET_SPENT; MAG FULL → REJECT MAGAZINE_FULL :767; QUAL MISS DOWN is one thing — refuse without spending, or count via qualRecordShot :2318; the AI seat's next mark waits for the HIT sentence's dwell :2506. Gates: SPENT_IS_A_ROW, extend RELOAD_ON_RECORD (full ⇒ ev+1), QUAL_OUT_OF_ENGAGEMENT_IS_ONE_THING, HIT_TOAST_READABLE (≥1400 ms).

HARDWARE-GATED: a real thumb on the HEAD stick (probes set pan/tilt); two physical phones over real WebRTC and their two clocks for LAPSE rows (host clock, lapseIsMine); the laser-tag receiver's hit vs pipOff<32 px; a live mic on "target one/two" (:3608 marks lockOn() as slot N); touch on the 11 px 150 R plate at 390 px; toast dwell on a phone GPU under load. Nothing in NOT HELD 1–7 needs hardware to reproduce.

### Athena

LENS: Athena · SYNTHESIS (A read-and-probed · B touch-only; both on r.147 sha 2476f68a; lines below cite docs/drone-2525/operator-deck/drone-2525_r.147.html)

STATE AT r.147 — the 60 seconds as lived (merged)
 0–6 s   CONTINUE ×11 (portrait) / SKIP TO SELECT (landscape) → CITY → CRAFT → RANGE · PRACTICE. He lands in a turret on lane 21 with the head parked ON the 50 L (goRange 4608 → rangeFirstPlate 803 → aimUnitAt 4626): pip 0 px from C-50-L21, strip "TARGET FIRST". The waiting room alone is a dead end ("ROOM HOLD · HOST STARTS", nothing names HOST ROOM).
 6–10 s  TARGET, stick untouched → "AMBER · 50 M · NOW PRESS APPROVE" → APPROVE → "RED BOX · 50 M · APPROVED BY YOU · NOW PRESS FIRE" → FIRE → "HIT · IN THE AIMING CIRCLE". First hit 7.3 s portrait / 4.4 s landscape — on the plate the deck chose.
 10–50 s the stick. Full deflection ≈37°/s portrait, ≈18°/s landscape; a 100–120 ms flick = 2.5–5.6°, the whole far cluster (50 L→300 spans ~3.2°; plates 10–40 px apart, an 11 px 150 m plate at 1×). No pan stop: 12 s of stick = −95°, empty picture, "NO TARGET UNDER THE BULLSEYE · PUT IT ON ONE" with no "which way". Look-nudge-look lands a named plate in 0.3–1.8 s.
 50–58 s with NO mark live, TARGET is honest: pip on it → marked it, head moves ≤0.4° (A 408/408 across 1×/3×, both orientations, RESET/DOWN/QUAL·40; B 10/11 plates — the 11th is the 300/200 R outline tie).
 THEN the complaint, verbatim, both orientations: 50 L amber (as parked) → stick, pip 4 px on the 150 R → TARGET → "AMBER · 50 M" and the head snaps 2–7.5° back to the 50 L → stick back → APPROVE reds the 50 L under a ring on the 150 R → FIRE → "MISS", a round spent, box never leaves the 50 L. Six APPROVE+FIRE = six MISS (B p5-portrait-08). Only exit a finger found: tap the plate on the picture → a SECOND slot T2, the 50 L still marked. No pill clears a mark; only a kill does (2379).

HELD: the words on every refusal (none silent) — grass/sky, ALL DOWN · RESET, QUAL gap, QUAL COMPLETE (1927); amber→red→HIT on the marked id 280/280; the fresh-mark pip rule (1929–1930, 1940); a finger on the plate marks it; double-tap/hold = one act; RELOAD full; rotation keeps state; FULL keeps TARGET·APPROVE·FIRE·RELOAD·EXIT top-most; landscape top lines disjoint; deck QA 34/0.
NOT HELD: N1 THE MARK OUTRANKS THE EYE (class) — lockOn 1934–1937 returns state.desig.ref inside a 35° cone (dot>0.82) BEFORE the pip rule; TARGET then aimUnitAt()s the head onto it (4393); REVISIONS.md:521 keeps it as a feature. The r.147 gates run with desig=null; the stranger never has desig=null after his first press. N2 FIRE scores "MISS" against a box 100+ px from the ring, spends the round, says nothing about where the box is (2380). N3 portrait top lines overprint whenever a slot is amber/red, 1× and 3×, 320–430 px wide. N4 minor: 300/200 R outline tie at 1× (1940, metres win); no pan stop / no pointer home; landscape bar row 2 off the right edge; MAP legend clipped.

DISAGREEMENTS SETTLED
 · Overlap source: A is right — the second line is DOM #board (list() 2397–2402, CSS line 37 top:44px) under #playHud top:42px (line 28); B read it as canvas text. The canvas bracket caption (1890) is suppressed in portrait. Same class either way; gate 4041 tests only #magBar vs #playHud, so it passes.
 · Stick rate: code is dt-scaled (2656–2657: `-lookX*120*dt*zf`); the portrait/landscape °/s gap is zf = fovDeg()/38 by design, not frame-binding. B's STICK_RATE_IS_SECONDS is withdrawn; B's "no pan stop / empty picture" stands.
 · Snap size: A 2.27° (100 C←150 R) vs B 3.2–7.5° (50 L←150 R/100 R) — different pairs, one bug.
 · Grade C vs D: D. The operator's sentence reproduces on a stranger's first minute in both orientations; "honest with no mark live" never applies to him.

### Odin

LENS: Odin · SYNTHESIS (A reasoned · B measured · disagreements verified against play.html == r.147)

STATE AT r.147
HOLDS AT SCALE (merged, A+B agree, verified)
- Reach is angular and DPR-invariant: LOCK_REACH_PX=48 (L1928) / focalPx (L786) = 26.26 mrad at 1×, identical at DPR 1/2/3 and every viewport (canvas backing = CSS px; no devicePixelRatio anywhere). 8.75 mrad @3×, 0.875 mrad @30×.
- The lock is cheap: lockOn 23–35 µs free / 0.5–1 µs marked, pipRank 617 candidates 0.18 ms, 2.17 lockOn per draw; ~1,400-plate scaled copy 65–98 µs. The wire carries id·kind·slot, never px (L1921) — screen size cannot fork the ledger.
BREAKS AT SCALE (merged; where A and B differed, the file decides)
- FPS: A's "40 fps at 8× throttle" was the deck's own state.fps; B's true rAF is 29.2 at 6× and 10.0 at 20× on the range unmarked. Verified L3735 `dt=Math.min(.05,…)` → state.fps ≥ 20 always, so liveHal's `fps<18 → HAL.PI` (L1040) is unreachable and calibrate's hold30 (L1054) reads 24 at a true 10. The budget is draw (750–4,700 proj/frame, 0.5–5 ms), not the lock. The number: <15 fps (Pi floor) at 20× CPU throttle, every range cell; Capital 15.8–22.6.
- THE AI MOVES THE HUMAN'S HEAD (verified L2495-2511): asmTick picks nearest IN METRES from the pit (L2504-2505), designates it ASM@ (L2510), then aimUnitAt(su,best) (L2511) swings the seated turret onto it; hysteresis (L1936, dot>0.82, no reach term) then keeps it. Pip 20 px beside the 150 R + ASM SPOT on → TARGET = C-50L. This is the operator's symptom re-entering through the AI door, in any room with an AI seat.
- TWO POOLS (verified L2192-2210): slots() ranks plates + pops only — no UAV/foil/ring/door — and at chNum()≠0 ranks the undrawn QUAL template; targetN checks reach for n===1 only, so keys 2/3 mark beyond the bullseye's reach.
- MOVERS/ZOOM: once marked, the 35° cone follows a UAV to the picture's edge (A, 737 px, 90 frames); unmarked, two 8.2 m/s crossers flip T1 4× in 60 frames. At 30× a Capital door 1,774 px tall is NONE 1 mrad off centre — non-plate kinds have only the 48 px centre radius (L1930), no outline pass (L1924 is plates only). Full stick crosses the reach in 2.6 frames at every zoom.
- RECORD: sample() logs lock id but not zoom·W·H; rangeTick is per device — a lock cannot be re-derived from its row, and two phones rank different standing plates.

GRADE at r.147: C+ — the rule is right, angular, cheap and DPR-proof, but it is one rule in one function: the AI seat, the T1·T2·T3 pool and the hysteresis each still run their own, and the deck's fps gauge cannot see the floor it falls under.

THE ONE SENTENCE
The bullseye rule you declared is correct and costs nothing at any scale in sight, but the AI member still marks by metres and physically turns your turret onto the 50 L, and T2/T3 still ignore the reach — that, not the rule, is why TARGET keeps resetting.

OWED r.148 (ordered by what "keeps resetting to 50 m left" needs first)
1. THE AI SPOTS THROUGH THE PIP, NEVER THE HAND — asmTick ranks by pipRank from the seated camera, and L2511 never moves a seated human's head unless hiLock is an opt-in. Gate: `ASM_NEVER_MOVES_THE_PIP` (pip 20 px beside the 150 R, ASM SPOT on → head 0.00°, mark = 150 R or none) + the 3v3 lobby row.
2. ONE POOL — lift lockOn's `cand` build out; slots() = pipRank(cand).slice(0,3); targetN checks reach for every n. Gate: `SLOTS_ARE_THE_LOCK_POOL` (T1..T3 ⊆ lockOn candidates; a UAV under the pip is T1 at CH2; no QUAL template from a Capital seat; key 2 beyond reach refuses).
3. THE MARK HOLDS ON THE PICTURE AND THE ROW REPLAYS IT — hysteresis bounded to on-picture within k·LOCK_REACH_PX (mover walks out → LOCK drops, record says so); a projected-outline pass for door/uav/ring/foil (+8 px, as plates get); sample() carries zoom·W·H; the r.137 EXPOSE row from the host. Gates: `LOCK_REPLAYS_FROM_THE_ROW` (100/100 rows) · `CAP_LOCK_AT_30X` (door centred, 1 mrad off → still the door) · two-phone MATCH.
ALSO OWED, off the complaint's path but provable headless: state.fps from unclamped wall-clock (L3735) so HAL.PI can engage under 18 (gate: throttled run, state.fps ≤ tickN/elapsed × 1.1); platesHere cached per frame (7.3 → ≤2 calls/draw, gate in range-2525).

HARDWARE-GATED
- Every fps above is headless Chromium under CDP throttle on a shared container; a Pi 4 GPU with a real 1080p stream and a DPR-3 phone on the bench decide the floor.
- 0.875 mrad reach at 30× vs a thumb: 2 px tremor crosses it in 0.7 s — only a held phone can say whether 30× is usable unmarked.
- Two-phone MATCH with the per-device range clock — needs two devices in one room.

### Pangu

LENS: Pangu · SYNTHESIS (A read asmTick; B played with it on) — deck play.html == docs/drone-2525/operator-deck/drone-2525_r.147.html (cmp SAME, sha 2476f68a…, BUILD.revision 0.147). Every cited line re-read; A and B do not contradict each other on any fact — they differ only in grade (C / C+) and which members of the class each enumerated. Merged below.

STATE AT r.147
HELD (gate)
- OFF is the default; cycle OFF→SPOT→FIRE→OFF — line 662 `asmSpot:false,asmFire:false`; #btnAsm 4432-4435. Gate: none by name (A+B both saw it in every run).
- Never fires on amber, once per box, only through fireN as ASM@device — 2513-2514. Gates ASM_FIRE_NEEDS_RED (4046), ASM_IS_AN_ACTOR (4064; covers DESIGNATED + HIT rows only).
- Never a qualification round — 2513 `state.rangeMode!=='qual40'`; A probe P5, B harness qual 6/6 by HI, mode swap releases the amber. NO GATE: no QA row; tests/range-2525.test.mjs mentions qual40 only for zoomMax/magCap, never asmTick.
- Never a dead / falling / down / off-lane plate — 2497 filter, 2509 guard, designate 1902-1903. Gates ASM_NEVER_MARKS_DEAD (4069), NO_MARK_ON_A_DEAD_TARGET.
- The mark is said and signed as THE AI (1918, seatWord 2311; DESIGNATED row role=AI via ev 2075). Human marks first → AI never overwrites (2509 `!state.desig`).
- Room: AI's own seat cannot approve its mark; other seat approves; marker's seat fires — 2277-2280. Gates AI_PASSPORT_NEVER_FROM_A_ROW (4076), AI_MARK_FIRES_IN_A_ROOM (4080); B replayed it on a DIRECT loopback, hashMatch=true.
NOT HELD (lines, repro)
1. TARGET takes the AI's amber over the pip. lockOn 1934-1936 returns state.desig.ref whenever it is in the 35° cone (dot>0.82) BEFORE pipNearest/pipRank 1929 runs; #fTgt 4393 then re-designates it BY THE HUMAN and aimUnitAt swings the head back. Repro (A P2, B P1): SPOT on → AI ambers C-50L → pip re-aimed on C-150R → TARGET → desig C-50L by SID, "MARKED BY THE AI" dropped, head yanked 5.26°. Key 1 marks the 150R (slots() has no hysteresis) — "key 1 marks what the button marks" is false with the AI on.
2. The AI marks nearest-in-metres from the pit, never the pip — 2502-2503 `Math.hypot(ow.x-pit.x, ow.z-pit.z)`. pangu-s walked 50L…300 in pure range order; on the sheet it re-marks the holed C-50 for ever (B P5). r.147 REVISIONS admits it.
3. The difference is erased, not said — 2511 `aimUnitAt(su,best,-40,20)` snaps the human's head on EVERY mark (7.9° / 119° with no word); a new mark and a new snap land in the same tick as each kill (A P3 <300 ms, B 23 ms) so the HIT sentence is never read — B's harness scored 3 "misses" while HIT went 0→3.
4. The AI's decisions are signed HI on the canonical record — decide() 2216-2221 stores actor=state._shooter but calls ev(kind,id,reason,extra) without `by`, so SIM-ACTION / REJECT EMPTY_MAGAZINE rows read role=HI while decisions[] says ASM@. Only netEvent 2084 passes the shooter. metricsOf 2103-2105 lumps ASM@ and SID designations.
5. Stale slot: the AI's amber survives key-1/kill and re-marks into a second slot — two rows for one plate (B P1 tgtSlot ["1:C-50L amber ASM","2:C-50L amber ASM"]).
6. After REJECT EMPTY_MAGAZINE the AI's one shot is spent — asmFired set at 2514 before fireN and never cleared (grep: no other writer; magReload 766 does not touch it) — box stays red, AI silent, no sentence.
7. `!state.hiLock` at 2509 is dead — hiLock is written false at 662, 2360, 2374, 2380, 4555 and never true; the human has no HOLD against the AI except AsM OFF. 2506-2507 also pan every other turret toward the seat's plate (cosmetic, no row).

GRADE at r.147: C — every safety rail on the AI holds and most have gates, but the AI's own amber captures the TARGET button, moves the head unsaid, and signs half its rows as the human.

THE ONE SENTENCE: Yes — with AsM SPOT on, "keeps resetting to 50 m left" reproduces on r.147 (A P2, B P1): the AI marks the 50 L by metres, and the TARGET button's hysteresis (1934-1936) then re-locks the AI's 50 L instead of the plate under your bullseye, so the r.147 pip rule never runs while an AI amber stands; with AsM OFF the r.147 fix holds.

OWED r.148 (ordered by the operator's complaint)
1. TARGET/LOCK with an AI amber up marks the pip's target: lockOn takes the desig hysteresis ONLY when nothing is within LOCK_REACH_PX of the pip (or never for an ASM@ mark); a human re-mark of another plate releases the AI's box with one row; key 1 and the button agree. Gate: QA row TARGET_MARKS_THE_PIP_OVER_THE_AI_AMBER (SPOT on, AI on 50 L, pip on 150 R → button marks 150 R by SID, head moves <1°, one RELEASE/HOLD row on the 50 L, key 1 agrees) + a range-2525 source regex.
2. The AI spots by the pip and says any difference: rank `live` with pipRank(cand,camOf(seat),W,H) (1929), fall back to nearest-in-metres only when nothing is on the picture, and then the toast says "NOT UNDER YOUR BULLSEYE · HEAD MOVED"; no re-mark for ≥1.2 s after a HIT/MISS toast; designate() reuses the slot holding the id. Gates: QA rows ASM_MARKS_THE_PIP, ASM_WAITS_FOR_THE_HIT_TO_BE_READ, ONE_AMBER_ROW_PER_PLATE.
3. The AI signs every row it causes and the refusals get gates: decide() passes state._shooter as data.by (SIM-ACTION/REJECT/HOLD role=AI); wireApprove keeps viaAI in the APPROVE row; metricsOf reports designationsAI/HI + shotsAI/HI; magReload clears asmFired (or the toast says "RELOADED · YOU FIRE THE RED BOX"); delete dead `!state.hiLock` or give it a HOLD button with a row. Gates: ASM_IS_AN_ACTOR extended to the SIM-ACTION row and an EMPTY_MAGAZINE REJECT by ASM@; QA rows ASM_NEVER_FIRES_QUAL (AsM FIRE, qual40, red 10 ticks → rangeHit unchanged) + range-2525 regex on `state.rangeMode!=='qual40'`; ASM_REARMS_ON_RELOAD; drone-deck-qa asserts the sidecar's AI fields.

HARDWARE-GATED: none of the above needs a device. A real joystick/touch-drag against the 7.9°/119° head snap at mark time (felt yank, latency); phone toast readability at 23 ms; WebRTC on two real phones (B's room was a loopback DIRECT path); the laser-tag rifle's own trigger vs the AI's fireN. On a real gimbal the snap becomes a safety complaint, so it must be a suggestion (paint the AI's box, do not move the head) before any mount is attached.

B · THE ACTOR'S SCREEN GETS THE MACHINE LINE (8 lines; B found, verified L1513/L1515, L1519/L1521, L813, L2350). D · THE RESULT OVERWRITTEN (B measured, verified L2126 + L2509). toast() replaces instantly; asmTick designates the moment !state.desig, so on the AI seat 'HIT · IN THE AIMING CIRCLE' lives 28 ms before 'AMBER · 50 M LEFT · MARKED BY THE AI …' — the shooter never sees the result and the harness scores every AI-seat hit as a miss. Same class, slower: the QUAL briefing L4575 (eight facts, one sentence) is replaced by 'RANGE · L21 DOWN · QUAL · 40' L4628 in the same call; key 2/3 flash 'MIST … · RED ON NET' (L2206) before the amber sentence and refuse nothing beyond reach (L2210 `n===1&&`).

THE TEN THAT MATTER (sentence → replacement)
1 strip 'AMBER T1 C-150R-L21' / 'RED T1 …' / 'LAST T1 … DOWN' → 'AMBER · 150 M RIGHT' / 'RED · 150 M RIGHT' / 'LAST · 150 M RIGHT · DOWN' (plateWord already exists)
2 board 'T1 AMBER C-150R-L21' → not rendered in portrait; the strip is the one writer
3 LOCK 'C-150R-L21 150M' → 'LOCK · 150 M RIGHT'
4 approver in a room 'NET AMBER C-150R-L08' → 'THE OTHER SEAT MARKED 150 M RIGHT · PRESS APPROVE'
5 marker in a room 'RED · PEER HI-2 · C-150R-L08' → 'RED BOX · 150 M RIGHT · APPROVED BY THE OTHER SEAT · NOW PRESS FIRE'
6 approver's own screen 'RED BOX · … · APPROVED BY THE OTHER SEAT · THE OTHER SEAT FIRES' → '… · APPROVED BY YOU · THE OTHER SEAT FIRES'
7 'THE SEAT THAT MARKED IT FIRES · YOU APPROVED' (before approving) → 'AMBER · PRESS APPROVE · THE SEAT THAT MARKED IT FIRES'
8 'YOUR TARGET LAPSED · MARK THE NEXT' (nothing was marked) → 'THE TARGET WENT DOWN UNMARKED · MARK THE NEXT'
9 AI seat 'HIT · IN THE AIMING CIRCLE' (28 ms) → holds ≥ 1400 ms, or 'HIT · 50 M LEFT · THE AI MARKS THE NEXT'
10 'QA 149/150' on a player's screen; intro 'Second HI turns amber red. AI may spot. HI authorizes.' → never toasted (console/?diag=1); 'A second person turns amber to red. The AI may spot. Only a person authorizes.'

GRADE at r.147: C — the one sentence the release promised is right at all three doors, but the strip, the board, the LOCK line and the room still speak the record, and the person who must act next is the one handed the machine line.

THE ONE SENTENCE for the operator: the deck has one good voice for the shot and three other voices for the same shot, and in a room the good voice goes to the wrong phone.

THE ONE SENTENCE: Your own trigger is gated nine ways; the other phone's word is not — it can sign your approval for you and knock your plates down without a red box, and the record will say you did it.

### MoT 3

MoT-3 · THE SSSES HONEST POSTURE (drone-spiral9 @ HEAD 10865bd r.148; scorer = frontend/scripts/drone-spiral9.mjs:238-278)

WHAT EACH PRINTED NUMBER IS
- Security 97 = 60 + round(372/10) (:247), 372 = passed checks of 4 gates (drone-flight, drone-link, si-pod, drone-authority). A CHECK COUNT with a 60 floor: green → 97 forever, one red → 0. Never 61-96. LABEL wearing a count. And the 372 live in lib/, not the deck's wire — Thor's spoofable `by` on APPROVE/HIT is outside them.
- Stability 100 = drift==0 && allGreen ? 100 : stable ? 70 : 40 (:254). Three-value label, but the predicate is a real invariant (12 artefacts byte-identical ×9). The one honest pillar.
- Scalability 0 = perf===null (:262): perf/after.json is stamped 85e8382, HEAD is 10865bd, the scorer refuses it (:321) and prints 0. UNMEASURED printed as zero.
- Efficiency 0 = same null (:271). UNMEASURED printed as zero.
- Succinctness 49 = 100 − 8 (hud×2) − 18 (9 dead exports) − 25 (Round 679 lines, capped) (:229). A MEASUREMENT — of lib/drone-2525 + lib/wire-core + components/drone-2525 only (:166). play.html (4,673 lines, the artefact that ships) is not in the surface.
- AVERAGE 49 = (97+100+0+0+49)/5 (:277): arithmetic over two zeros that mean "not run". And SSSES is NOT in the PASS line (:337: allGreen && !drift && spiralOk) — the block decorates, it never gates.
- The deck's own row SSSES (play.html:4127) is `push('SSSES', S.avg>0)`: sec = red box ? 100 : 95, suc = 70+20+10, scale = 70+peers×15, stab = 100−crash×20 (:2620-2636). A tautology that cannot fail; the register already says "SSSES retired until measured" (REVISIONS.md:324) yet the row and the sS/sT/sC/sE/sU/sA readout still run.

WHAT EACH SHOULD READ AT r.148, AND THE SCRIPT + NUMBER
- Security → "372/372 authority checks (4 gates); deck wire not in scope". Drop the 60 floor: score = round(100×passed/total) of those gates, or print the fraction and no score. Read: spiral9 gateRuns[0][secGates].passed.
- Stability → 100 as now, plus the boot QA beside it: "160/161 rows × 2 orientations, red = SYNC_DIRECT (offline)". Read: `npm run test:drone-deck-qa` → perf/deck-qa.json (stamp it with the commit as after.json is; refuse a stale one the same way).
- Scalability → UNMEASURED (null) until `npm run test:drone-perf` (scripts/drone-perf.mjs, PERF_CPU=10, 6 s window) is re-run at HEAD; then round(fps/30×100) from after.json runs["42 aircraft 2.3"].fps. Last real reading, 85e8382: 42.8 fps → 100.
- Efficiency → UNMEASURED (null) from the same file: 55×min(1,fps/30) + 25·memoHit + 20·(domNodes<260). Last real reading: 216 nodes, memo HOLDS → 100. (Odin: state.fps is clamped ≥20 at play.html:3735 — the DECK's fps gauge is not this number; drone-perf counts rAF frames itself.)
- Succinctness → 49 with its surface named ("lib+components; play.html unscored"); put play.html in readSource() and let it fall.
- AVERAGE → over measured pillars only, printed as "avg of 3/5 measured", or dropped. An unmeasured pillar in deploy.yml (where drone-perf runs in no workflow — Enlil) turns the PASS line to FAIL, not to 0.
- Deck row SSSES → delete, or make it read perf/deck-qa.json + after.json shipped as data; a row that asserts avg>0 is not a row.

HOW THE 161 ROWS AND THE FLEET ENTER THE SCORE
- The 161-row boot QA is Stability evidence and only Stability: it runs in headless Chromium after push to main (deploy.yml:71), never in test:ci, never in spiral9's GATES. Add test:drone-deck-qa to spiral9 (browser present) or read its commit-stamped JSON; print rows/red beside Stability. Its notes are counted, its predicates are not (Enlil: 8 of 161 note-checked) — 160/161 is a set-equality count, not 160 behaviours.
- The fleet's findings enter as ROWS, never as points: each owed gate (TARGET_BUTTON_MARKS_THE_PIP, ASM_NEVER_MOVES_THE_PIP, WIRE_APPROVE_AUTHOR_FROM_ENVELOPE, REJECT_NEVER_NAMES_AN_UNMARKED_TARGET…) becomes a manifest row and raises the denominator; until it is green it is a red the operator sees. Twelve grades D..A− with a median C are the honest state; print "open defects: N (register r.148)" under the block, no pillar absorbs them.
- Security's 97 must not be read as "the deck's authority is 97% proven": Thor (claimed `by` trusted), Pangu (AI signs rows as HI) and Asar (REJECT names an unmarked plate) are authority defects none of the 372 checks can see.

THE ONE LINE THE COMMIT MAY HONESTLY CARRY
SSSES @10865bd (drone-spiral9 ×9, 4355 checks, 0 drift): Security 372/372 authority checks (lib only) · Stability 100 (12 artefacts ×9 byte-identical; boot QA 160/161 ×2, red=SYNC_DIRECT offline) · Scalability UNMEASURED (perf/after.json is 85e8382) · Efficiency UNMEASURED · Succinctness 49 (lib+components; play.html unscored) — no average; 12-lens median C, defects open in register r.148.

### MoT 4

MoT-4 · THE TWO-PHONE MATCH CLAIM at r.148 (play.html == drone-2525_r.148.html; lines cite r.148; Christo/Thor SYNTH + the 10 others read; patch r147_to_r148.py §7 and drone-team-e2e.mjs re-read; run log 9/9, hash a898aba0 == a898aba0)

WHAT "ONE HASH, MATCH ON BOTH" PROVES — replayHash :2094 = fnv1a64 over rows with committed!==false, sorted by orderKey, fields eventId·orderKey·sessionSeq·logicalClock·peerId·seq·verb·id·result·data·blu·red. So equal hashes prove: the two COMMITTED ledgers hold the same rows in the same host order. MATCH (:1457/:1498/:1560/:1568) is a point sample: "the hash the peer computed when it sent equals mine now".
WHAT IT DOES NOT PROVE — (a) that the rows are TRUE (a row a peer signed honestly for a plate nobody marked hashes like any other); (b) that the two WORLDS agree (plate up/down, lifePct, _eng, rangeHit/Miss/Lapsed, desig phase and the two strips are not hashed — applyWorld may refuse on one phone and apply on the other under one hash); (c) that the two heads are EQUAL (a joiner's pending row is excluded from its hash :2095, so MATCH is reported while its HOLD/HIT is still in flight — a false MATCH the e2e never checks: pendingSeq and sessionSeq are noted, not asserted); (d) that the sample was taken at the same sessionSeq (see DIVERGED below); (e) anything about `t` — the per-device clock is not in the hash, by design, and the world it drives is not either.

WIRE CHANGE 1 · APPROVE AUTHOR (:1518 `by=m.peerId||m.sid||m.by||pay.by`) — NARROWED, NOT CLOSED. It defeats Thor R5 as written (commEnv stamps peerId=SID :1398, so `by` is now ignored when peerId is present). It does not close the class: the fallback `||m.by||pay.by` survives, and NOTHING binds the inner peerId to the channel — lcOpen :3260 verifies AES-GCM with the OUTER w.peerId in AAD and hands commIn the INNER object unstamped; :1411 DROPS (no row) an inner peerId===SID instead of refusing it; the room key is symmetric, so "authenticated sender" today means "someone holding the room key", which in 1v1 is exactly the other phone. Repro left open: joiner seals `{k:'APPROVE',id,by:hostSid}` with no peerId/sid → not dropped at :1411 → by=hostSid → wireApprove :2255 → members[hostSid] && hostSid!==d.by → RED on the host, APPROVE row approvedBy=host the host never made — Thor R5 with one field deleted. The QA row Thor named (WIRE_APPROVE_AUTHOR_FROM_ENVELOPE) is not in r.148 (manifest has none; only WIRE_APPROVE_TWO_HUMANS, which calls wireApprove directly and never crosses the wire). Close: commIn takes the author from the channel (receiver-stamped `state.codex.peer` / the DC's member), refuses an envelope whose inner peerId is absent or ≠ the channel's with a REJECT UNSIGNED_ENVELOPE row, and deletes the `by` fallback; 2v2 needs per-seat keys or signatures before "authenticated sender" is true there.

WIRE CHANGE 2 · PEER HIT (:2009) — NARROWED, NOT CLOSED, AND THE REFUSAL IS OFF THE RECORD. (i) The row is committed BEFORE the world check: appendCanonicalEvent pushes, hashes and only then applyWorld :2072 — so after a forged HIT both ledgers carry `HIT C-100L by joiner`, one hash, MATCH on both; the joiner's plate is down and its strip reads HIT +1; the host's plate stands and its strip reads HIT 0. Equal, not honest. (ii) `log('REJECT',…)` :2132 writes state.replay only — not state.events, not decisions, not hashed, not sent; the joiner never learns it was refused and nothing on either record says the HIT was ignored. Using decide('REJECT',id,{reason:'HIT_WITHOUT_RED_BOX'}) instead keeps the records equal (host commits, SEQ_COMMIT/envelope carries it, joiner appends → same hash after one round trip) AND honest (HIT then REJECT, in host order). Log-only refusal buys equality by hiding the dissent. (iii) The guard is "ANY APPROVE row ever for this id" — in bounce mode a plate returns and the first honest approval of C-100L licenses forged HITs on C-100L for the rest of the session; a RESET mid-red does not retire it either. Bind it to the current engagement: an APPROVE with sessionSeq > the last RESET/RELEASE row for that id and no later HIT/LAPSE for it. (iv) Legitimate ordering race: in 1v1 there is none — the HIT's receiver is always the approver, whose own APPROVE row (pending or committed) precedes its ability to send APPROVE, and DC delivery is ordered. It appears with a THIRD seat (2v2: HIT envelope from the shooter can land before the approver's APPROVE reaches the third phone), after a SNAPSHOT replay (:1541 folds host array order, not orderKey), and when the host RESET crosses a joiner's FIRE in flight (stale APPROVE passes, plate downed after reset — same hash, HIT 1 on both, world consistent by luck of fold order). A refusal on the honest race must therefore be a HOLD that re-applies when the APPROVE lands, never a silent drop. QA row PEER_HIT_NEEDS_RED_ON_RECORD :3964 asserts only up/life/rangeHit — not that a REJECT row exists; it would pass a silent drop.

FALSE DIVERGED — SEQ_COMMIT/HELLO/ACK compare the peer's hash-at-ITS-head with my hash-at-MY-head (:1457/:1498/:1568) and adopt sessionSeq only as a max. Christo's window (127–1161 ms after a host RESET, hashes equal) is a joiner ACK whose hash predates its own pending HOLD that the host has already committed. r.148 patch touches none of it. Fix: every hash on the wire carries the sessionSeq it was taken at; the receiver hashes its rows with sessionSeq ≤ that seq (replayHash(atSeq)) and compares equal-seq hashes; an ACK/HELLO with seq < mine is compared at its seq, never at mine. Same primitive kills the false MATCH: MATCH only when pendingSeq is empty on both and heads are equal.

PER-DEVICE RANGE CLOCK — state.clock += dt per device, dt clamped at .05 :2649 (a loaded phone's clock runs slow against the wall), rangeTick(dt) raises/returns plates on each clock; only LAPSE is host-owned (lapseIsMine :811). Christo S6 measured 236 ms of lead and one sample with H red on a standing plate while J's plate was down. Consequences the hash cannot see: a joiner FIRE at the window edge → joiner HIT row + host LAPSE row for one plate, both committed, one hash, strips disagree; RETURN_S returns a plate on one phone first, it gets marked, approveDesig skips TARGET_DOWN for PEER :2281, the shooter's HIT lands on a plate the other phone still shows down. Fix: plate rise/return/window-end are host rows (EXPOSE/RETURN/WINDOW, like LAPSE) and the joiner's rangeTick folds them; until then the e2e must hash the WORLD too.

r.149 · drone-team-e2e.mjs STEPS (append after step 9; every hash assertion becomes: pendingSeq.length===0 on both && sessionSeq equal && replayHash equal && worldDigest equal, where worldDigest = JSON of platesHere().map(id,up,lifePct|0,_eng) + rangeHit/Miss/Lapsed + desig{id,phase,by} + #phScore text):
 10 FORGED APPROVE ×3 — J marks (J pip on a plate, #fTgt). (a) J.evaluate linkSend({k:'APPROVE',id,by:hostSid}) (no peerId/sid/event); (b) linkSend(Object.assign(commEnv('APPROVE',{id,by:hostSid}),{by:hostSid})); (c) commEnv('APPROVE',{id,event:{…hostRowShape,peerId:hostSid,verb:'APPROVE',id}}). Assert on H after 600 ms: desig.phase==='amber', hiApproved false, a COMMITTED row on H with verb REJECT|HOLD and reason ∈ {UNSIGNED_ENVELOPE, TWO_HUMANS, ROW_AUTHOR_MISMATCH}, no APPROVE row for id on either record, the same reason row on J, then the full 4-part match.
 11 FORGED HIT — J.evaluate: const row={eventId:SID+'-X1',seq:999,peerId:SID,verb:'HIT',id:<standing unmarked plate on H's lane>,result:'HIT CIRCLE',data:{}}; linkSend(Object.assign(commEnv('HIT',{id:row.id,event:row}),{id:row.id,event:row})). Assert on H: plate up, lifePct 100, _eng falsy, rangeHit unchanged, #phScore unchanged, a COMMITTED REJECT HIT_WITHOUT_RED_BOX row in state.events (not state.replay); on J: the same REJECT row and a toast naming it; then the 4-part match — and assert H's and J's #phScore texts are equal (today they differ under one hash).
 12 STALE APPROVAL DOES NOT LICENSE — after step 9's honest HIT on plate P, wait RETURN_S for P to stand again on both; J sends step 11's forged HIT for P. Assert refused as in 11 (today it passes: the old APPROVE row for P satisfies :2009).
 13 PEER MARK KEEPS ITS AUTHOR — J marks Q (pip on Q); H presses #fTgt without moving (H's head was turned onto Q by peerDesig :2252). Assert: H.desig.by===joinerSid, how 'PEER', H events length unchanged (no DESIGNATED, no re-sign), toast /THE OTHER SEAT MARKED IT/; H APPROVE → red both; J FIRE → HIT on both strips; 4-part match. Variant: H moves its pip onto R and presses #fTgt → H marks R under hostSid in a second slot, Q still J's; no releaseAuthority row for Q.
 14 NO FALSE DIVERGED / NO FALSE MATCH — mid-red H clicks #btnRangeReset; sample {sync,sessionSeq,pending,hash} on both every 50 ms for 1.5 s. Assert: no sample reads DIVERGED; no sample reads MATCH while pending>0 or heads differ; final 4-part match. Repeat after a joiner HOLD (J marks another lane's plate → PEER_MARK_OTHER_LANE) and after J.reload mid-red. 10 runs in CI, 0 tolerance.
 15 ONE CLOCK — at every checkpoint above assert worldDigest equal on both phones; add a qual40 leg: J switches mode, J marks, H approves, J fires at the 5 s edge (rangeTick driven to the window end on J first); assert that no plate is HIT on one record and LAPSE on the other — until the host EXPOSE/RETURN rows exist this step is the proof the claim is still open, and it stays red.
 Placement: drone-team-e2e runs only in deploy.yml:72 (after push); r.149 puts it, or a loopback-only subset of 10–14, on test:ci.

GRADE r.148 (this question): C — the naive forgery and the naive peer HIT are stopped, but the author still comes from the message not the channel, the HIT refusal is written where no hash and no peer can see it, the ledger is committed before it is judged, and the gate that says MATCH still compares two different heads and ignores the world.
THE ONE SENTENCE: One hash on both phones proves the two ledgers are identical, not that they are true or that the two pictures agree — and r.148 kept them identical by refusing in a log the hash never reads.

# MoT-7 — WHAT ONLY HARDWARE CAN PROVE · THE OPERATOR'S PHONE SCRIPT FOR r.148
Sources: 12 SYNTH HARDWARE-GATED sections (Enlil, Thor: none owed beyond the range; Krishna: none) + REVISIONS.md ## r.148. Strings below are verbatim from play.html r.0.148.

## A. Hardware-gated, deduplicated (headless Chromium stands in for none)
1. THUMB vs 11 px plate: the ring must sit inside outline+8 px (LOCK_REACH 48 px) on a 390 px screen at real fps — Asar, Aset, Athena, Odin.
2. TREMOR at 30×: 0.875 mrad reach; 2 px jitter crosses it in 0.7 s, flip rate/s on the 200 L/R tie — Enki, Odin.
3. iOS pointer capture on #joyR (3637-41); real pinch (3675) and rotation (1143); Fullscreen API — Athena, Christo, Enki.
4. DSF 3 legibility: 10 px #F0A020 strip on black in daylight, board-under-strip geometry, 45 ms/char toast dwell under GPU load — Sofia, Asar, Athena.
5. Real WebRTC/NAT on Wi-Fi: DIRECT/AUTHORIZED, T-number and APPROVE sentence on the joiner, "THE OTHER SEAT IS GONE" within 6 s, false DIVERGED ~1 s after HOLD, per-device range clock (LAPSE rows) — Aset, Sofia, Christo, Thor, Odin, Pangu.
6. Real mic: "target one/two" as ONE commit (:3608/:3623), grammar under real speech — Asar, Aset, Sofia, Thor.
7. Pi 4 fps with a 1080p stream: HAL.PI engages only when fps<18 (:1040); DPI is DECLARED not read, so px-per-metre is iPhone-12-Pro-Max-only — Odin, Thoth.
8. THE PRE-AIM ON ENTRY: head parked on the 50 m plate; a real gimbal/joystick yank when a mark moves the head (r.148: only after a mark, never by the AI) — Pangu, Aset.
9. Physical trigger/laser → KeyF mapping, debounce, receiver hit vs pipOff<32 px; plate sizes vs the pop-up range — Thor, Asar, Thoth.

## B. THE SCRIPT — one phone, then two. Precondition: deck Verify Live green on r.148 (play.html BUILD 0.148).
URL: https://exel-ai-polling.explore-096.workers.dev/drone-2525/play.html — portrait first, repeat 10-14 in landscape.
1. Open. PRACTICE, AsM button reads "AsM OFF". FAIL: board text overprinting the 2-line strip (top 74 px must clear it).
2. Press TARGET without touching the stick. PASS: "AMBER · 50 M LEFT · NOW PRESS APPROVE" (the parked plate). FAIL: anything else marked.
3. Thumb the right stick until the ring sits on the 150 R. Press TARGET. PASS: "AMBER · 150 M RIGHT · NOW PRESS APPROVE"; the head does NOT jump back to the 50 L; strip/board shows one HOLD row (RE-MARK) for the 50 L. FAIL: "AMBER · 50 M LEFT" again or the head snapping back = the complaint is alive.
4. Press TARGET again on the same plate. PASS: "AMBER · 150 M RIGHT · PRESS APPROVE" (same box, no new HOLD row).
5. APPROVE → "RED BOX · …"; FIRE with the ring still on it. PASS: "HIT …" and 150 R falls; MAG down by 1. FAIL: MISS on a plate you were on.
6. Mark the 300 (TARGET → AMBER 300 M), APPROVE, then swing the ring off it, press TARGET on empty grass. PASS: "NO TARGET UNDER THE BULLSEYE · PUT IT ON ONE", red box KEPT (a red box is never demoted). FAIL: box lost or another plate marked.
7. FIRE with the ring off the red 300. Known r.149 gap: it fires today. RECORD the sentence and MAG; PASS for r.148 = it never names a plate nobody marked.
8. RESET. Press FIRE with no box. PASS: "NO RED BOX · MARK A TARGET FIRST"; ?diag=1 record shows REJECT NONE. FAIL: a plate name in the REJECT row.
9. Press key/voice "target two" (or say it). PASS: T2 only if a plate is inside the reach, else "NO T2"; head does not move on refusal.
10. AsM button → "AsM SPOT · THE AI MARKS, YOU APPROVE AND FIRE". Hold the ring beside the 150 R. PASS within ~2 s: "AMBER · 150 M RIGHT · MARKED BY THE AI" and the head does NOT move (0.000°). FAIL: "… · NOT UNDER YOUR BULLSEYE" while your ring IS on a plate, or the head swinging.
11. With the AI's amber up, put the ring on the 100 C, press TARGET. PASS: "AMBER · 100 M CENTRE" — your pip wins; the AI's box is not re-signed by you. FAIL: "THE AI MARKED IT · PRESS APPROVE" for the 150 R.
12. APPROVE, FIRE → "HIT …". PASS: no new AMBER for ≥1.2 s (you can read HIT). FAIL: the AI re-marks in the same frame.
13. AsM → "AsM FIRE". Mark, APPROVE. PASS: the AI fires, sentence names the plate; you never pressed FIRE. RESET → no courtesy carry-over (AI marks at once).
14. Zoom to 30× (pinch), ring on the 200 R with the 200 L beside it. Hold 5 s. PASS: LOCK line does not flip L/R more than once per second with a still thumb. RECORD flips/s (item A2).
15. ?diag=1: note fps mean/min. PASS on the Pi/phone: fps ≥ 18 (HAL.PI never engages on a phone; below 18 is the Pi floor, record it).
TWO PHONES (real Wi-Fi, no hotspot loopback):
16. A: HOST ROOM → TEAM SECRETS code to B. B: JOIN OFFER / CREATE ANSWER → A: HOST APPLY ANSWER. Both READY; A START. PASS: DIRECT then AUTHORIZED on both.
17. B pinches to 3× and swings 90° off. A: ring beside 150 R, TARGET. PASS A: "AMBER · 150 M RIGHT · WAIT FOR THE OTHER SEAT TO APPROVE"; B: "… MARKED BY THE OTHER SEAT", B's head does NOT move.
18. A APPROVE → "TWO HUMANS · A SECOND PERSON MUST APPROVE · THE OTHER SEAT PRESSES APPROVE". B APPROVE → red on both. A FIRE → "HIT …" on A; "THE OTHER SEAT HIT · 150 M RIGHT" on B. MORE on both: hashes equal, NET MATCH.
19. THE COMPLAINT, two seats: B marks the 250; A presses TARGET on the 50 L. PASS: 250 stays B's (A reads "THE OTHER SEAT MARKED IT · PRESS APPROVE" only if A's ring is ON the 250; on the 50 L A gets its own AMBER 50 M LEFT and B keeps the 250). FAIL: B's box re-signed as A's or B's FIRE saying "YOU APPROVED".
20. A RESET mid-red; open ?diag=1 on A within 1 s. PASS: NET MATCH, equal hashes. RECORD any DIVERGED flash and how long (Christo gap, r.149).
21. B kills the browser mid-red. PASS on A within ~1-6 s: "THE OTHER SEAT IS GONE · YOU ARE ALONE", red box amber again, FIRE refused. Note B cannot rejoin without a new offer.
22. B pretends a hit via a stale tab (or replays MORE): A must show "THE OTHER SEAT REPORTED A HIT ON A TARGET NOBODY APPROVED · IGNORED" and no plate falls.
Report: phone model, DSF, orientation, steps 3/10/17/19 sentences verbatim, fps, flips/s at 30×. Any FAIL at 3, 10, 17 or 19 = r.148 does not close the complaint on hardware.

### MoT 8

MoT-8 · THE RECORD'S AUTHORSHIP LAW · deck r.148 (drone-2525_r.148.html, BUILD 0.148; lines cited there; read after Asar/Pangu/Aset + 9 SYNTHs)

LAW (one sentence): A row is one ev() call (:2076) appended to state.events and folded into replayHash (:2094) — eventId SID-E<seq>, peerId = the device that pressed, role = AI iff data.by starts ASM@ else HI, verb·id·result·data — and every act that changes a box, a magazine, a plate or a tally, and every refusal of such an act, is a row that names its actor; what reaches only log()/state.replay (:2132) or toast() (:2131) is not on the record.
Corollary at r.148: "by" lives in data.by (DESIGNATED, AI HIT/MISS, peer refusals) or approvedBy/designatedBy (APPROVE); decide() (:2228) keeps actor only in decisions[] and passes NO by to ev(), so every HOLD/REJECT/RESET/RELOAD/SIM-ACTION row is signed by the device (peerId) and reads role=HI even when the AI caused it.

CLOSED at r.148 (from the r.147 OWED list): boxless FIRE → NONE (13); RE-MARK is a row (3); wire APPROVE author from the envelope (10); peer HIT needs a red box (26, log-only); AI mark by the pip, head unmoved, HIT dwell (22). Still OPEN: every MARK/APPROVE-time refusal (4, 5, 9), SPENT (15), RELOAD FULL (18), QUAL MISS DOWN (16), scene/lane strip zero (21), AI refusals (24), and the by-less decide() (rows 3, 11, 12, 23) — the record still tells the truth about every shot and stays silent on every refusal at the mark and the approve.
GRADE r.148: B− (r.147 C+) — the two rows the operator's complaint manufactured (a REJECT naming a plate nobody marked; a re-mark with no trace) are fixed and gated; the authorship law itself is one line from held: decide() must pass by.

### MoT 9

MoT 9 · 3v3 READINESS — ordered prerequisites before "3v3 on the live site" may be claimed (six humans, three aircraft a side)
Sources: Odin/Christo/Enki SYNTH first, then the other nine; plan §P2-7 (L1888-1913, L2017-2035, L2337); REVISIONS §r.148; play.html == r.148 (lines cited).
VERDICT: NOT claimable. r.148 fixed the eye-vs-mark class on ONE seat and the 1v1 wire; it touched none of the seven 3v3 prerequisites. Topology is one host + one joiner (state.link = one pc/one dc, L3290/L3326; linkSend writes one dc, L2074+). 9v9 is already refused in code (L3034 '9v9 LOCKED'); 3v3 is not, and would silently run 1v1 transport under a 3-per-team roster (lobbyCapacity 1..9, L2857).

ORDER (each blocks the next; a claim needs all seven green plus the hardware row)
1. ADDRESSING (craftId/matchId · humanSeatsFor) — EXISTS: nothing. grep craftId|matchId in play.html = 0; perSide lives only in lib/contest.ts (batch ladder) and the 42-swarm stub (L4338); the roster carries peerId/team/craft/lane (L2934) but rows carry peerId only. r.148: unchanged. GATE: every DESIG/APPROVE/HIT/LAPSE row carries {matchId, craftId, seat}; a two-match, six-context harness reduces to two disjoint hashes; a row addressed to another craft is a HOLD (WRONG_CRAFT), never applied. HW: none.
2. TRANSPORT FAN-OUT (one pc/dc PER PEER) — EXISTS: no. HOST/JOIN offer-answer paste is pairwise (L3318-3398); a third phone has no door. r.148: unchanged. GATE: drone-team-e2e extended to N=6 isolated contexts, host relays or meshes, every seat sees every committed row, ONE hash on six phones, MATCH×6. HW: six handsets over real Wi-Fi/NAT (STUN only, L3324; no TURN).
3. SESSION ORDERING (orderKey in the hash) — EXISTS: closed further than the plan says. sessionOrderKey = sessionSeq:peer:seq (L1180); host sequencer stamps sessionSeq (L1192-1201); replayHash SORTS by orderKey (L2095-2100) — plan L2017 "replayHash doesn't sort by it" is stale. Gates SESSION_ORDER_STRUCT + HOST_SEQUENCER (L4115-4120). r.148: unchanged. OPEN: two concurrent originators are only proven with one joiner; Christo's false DIVERGED (ACK hash over uncommitted HOLD, L2090 vs L1466) becomes 5× likelier with 5 joiners. GATE: six seats fire TARGET within 50 ms → identical sorted log on all six, no DIVERGED sample 100-1200 ms after any HOLD/RESET, 10 runs. HW: none.
4. PER-PEER LIVENESS — EXISTS: single clock. state.com.lastPeerAt is one scalar (L1423/L1493); silence 12 s → linkLost, peers=1, ALONE (L1593-1597); LOST_APPROVER_IS_SAID (L4084) covers one approver. r.148: unchanged. GATE: per-peer lastSeen map; one seat killed mid-red → only ITS approval is withdrawn ("SEAT 4 IS GONE"), the other four stay DIRECT, hash unmoved; rejoin without a new offer. HW: a phone actually killed/backgrounded on Wi-Fi (iOS suspends dc).
5. CANONICAL RANGE CLOCK / HOST EXPOSE ROW — EXISTS: no. grep EXPOSE row = 0; rangeTick runs per device from RANGE_RUN[lane] (L797/L817); only the LAPSE row is host-owned via lapseIsMine (L811). Two phones already rank different standing plates (Odin). r.148: unchanged (r.137-owed item 1 still owed). GATE: host emits EXPOSE|{ids,t} per engagement, joiners raise from the row, never their own timer; six seats' exposed sets equal at every tick; a joiner's stalled tab still shows the host's plates. HW: none (six-context harness); real clock skew only on handsets.
6. AI SEAT IN A ROOM — EXISTS: asmTick marks via pipRank from the SEATED camera, why='METRES' fallback says NOT UNDER YOUR BULLSEYE, never aimUnitAt the seat, 1.2 s hold after a shot (L2507-2530, L1919); signs 'ASM@'+SID (one per device); fires only on red, never in QUAL. r.148: CHANGED (this is the r.148 class, gates ASM_SPOTS_THE_PIP_AND_NEVER_MOVES_IT, TARGET_OVER_AI_AMBER_MARKS_THE_PIP, AI_WAITS_FOR_THE_HIT_TO_BE_READ; asm-play seat 'ai' looks at the box before approving). OPEN for 3v3: six devices = six ASM@ authors marking independently with no arbitration; hiLock is per-device; Pangu: the AI signs SIM-ACTION/REJECT as HI. GATE: in an N-seat room at most one ASM@ box per lane, its author's SID on every row it produces, HI OVER AI (operator decision, MoT 10) enforced as a HOLD row. HW: none.
7. FPS INSTRUMENT — EXISTS: broken by construction. dt=Math.min(.05,…) (L3749) → state.fps ≥ 20 always; HAL.PI (fps<18, L1040) unreachable; hold30 (L1054) reads 24 at a true 10; sssesDevice eff from the same number (L2634). r.148: unchanged, named owed. GATE: state.fps from unclamped wall-clock; under CDP 20× throttle state.fps ≤ tickN/elapsed×1.1 and HAL.PI engages; drone-perf publishes fpsP05 per seat. HW: Pi 4 + 1080p stream, DPR-3 phone — the floor is a device number, not a container number.
8. STICK AT 30× — EXISTS: rate zf=fovDeg()/38 (L2669, r.141) so a full stick crosses the picture in the same seconds at any zoom, i.e. crosses LOCK_REACH_PX (48 px = 0.875 mrad at 30×) in 2.6 frames (Odin); 2 px tremor = the whole reach in 0.7 s; hysteresis pipOn +8/28 px is the only hold. r.148: pipOn hold added (L1931); rate unchanged. GATE: headless — drag 1 px at 30× keeps LOCK (pipOn); flick then release settles on the plate. HW-ONLY: a thumb at 30× on a 6.1" screen — flips/s, usable-or-not, and the outline pass for door/uav/ring/foil (Odin: Capital door 1 mrad off = NONE) need a held phone.

WHAT r.148 BOUGHT 3v3: only #6 (the AI no longer moves a seated head — the one fix that scales, since six AIs moving six heads was the worst case) and the wire's APPROVE author from the envelope (L1518). Everything else on this list is where r.147 left it.
CLAIM LADDER (words allowed on the site): today "1v1 · two phones · one record" (team-e2e 9/9, headless). "3v3" only after #1-#5 gates are in test:ci AND one six-handset session on real Wi-Fi returns six equal hashes with #7's fps honest. Never "9v9" (locked in code, correctly).

### MoT 10

MOT-10 · THE OPERATOR DECISIONS OWED (fleet147 12 syntheses + REVISIONS.md r.147/r.148 + plan; r.148 is shipped at 10865bd, so four of these are "confirm or reverse what the engineer already took"; play.html lines = r.148). Ordered by the work that waits on each.

1. DOES THE PICTURE GATE THE ACT? — should APPROVE refuse when the target is off the approver's picture, and FIRE refuse when the ring is off the red box? (Christo B#3 deferred; Thor 4/6 carried to r.149; Athena owed 2; register r.148 "still open": blind approval, FIRE_SAYS_WHERE; folds Thor 6 "one approval = N pulls at CH1–4" :2392.)
   (a) GATE — approveDesig/wireApprove HOLD TARGET_NOT_ON_PICTURE, fireN REJECT TARGET_OFF_PICTURE spending nothing, MAP hides FIRE, a pull on a held red is a row. Cost: peerDesig aims turrets only (:2240) so a quad approver must be turned or exempted; a 1× joiner may need 3× to see the plate; qaArmed rows rework; second pull after a miss needs the ring back on.
   (b) AIM ONLY (as r.133 wrote it) — cost: a joiner 70° off approves; MISS keeps red so one approval covers N pulls with the ring anywhere; the record carries approvals of plates the approver never saw.
   DEFAULT: FIRE gated (invariant: no round spent on a box the ring is not on — Athena 2), APPROVE stays an aim + one sentence until the two-phone script proves the aim lands; N-pulls stays doctrine with a row per pull.
2. THE DEPLOY DOOR — set NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL / ANON_KEY (+ CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN / ACCOUNT_ID) in Actions so Deploy actually builds, or retire that half and gate Cloudflare Workers Builds alone? (Krishna 1/3, Enlil 3, plan :2751; deploy.yml:80-86 skips Build/Deploy on empty secrets and reports success.)
   (a) SET THE SECRETS — cost: two shipping doors (Actions + Workers Builds) unless one is switched off; deploy timeout-minutes 20 must grow for spiral9 (314 s) + deck-qa; secrets live in GitHub.
   (b) RETIRE THE STEP — cost: Cloudflare's build command must be confirmed as `npm run build` in the dashboard (Build d8893002…) so pre/postbuild fire; its container may lack git, so the tmpdir memo stamps production unless SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH is set as a build var.
   DEFAULT: engineer cannot touch either door; he adds `postbuild` = render-smoke + one-stamp count, marks the skipped Deploy job neutral, moves deck-qa into test:ci — both doors then run the same gates whichever you pick.
3. THE PRE-AIM ON RANGE ENTRY — goRange parks the head ON the 50 (rangeFirstPlate :803, :4651; lane change :4630), and the first TARGET marks it: this is where "50 m left" begins (Athena 0–10 s; register r.148 §1). Keep it?
   (a) KEEP — first hit in 4–7 s on the plate the deck chose; r.148 releases that mark the moment the pip leaves it (TARGET_FOLLOWS_THE_EYE), so the cost is now only that the first mark is never the shooter's choice.
   (b) ENTER ON THE GRASS (or pre-aim, no mark, say "PUT THE BULLSEYE ON ONE") — cost: the stranger's first sentence is a refusal; no pan stop exists (12 s of stick = −95°, Athena N4); RANGE_LOCK_PLATE / TARGETN_HITS_THE_EXPOSED_PLATE / CH0_PIT rows rework.
   DEFAULT: keep the pre-aim; add the pan stop / "RANGE ←" on an empty picture (engineer's).
4. MAY THE AI MARK BY METRES WHEN NOTHING IS UNDER THE PIP? r.148 took: pipRank from the seated camera, metres fallback flagged `_asmWhy='METRES'` (:2516-2518) with "NOT UNDER YOUR BULLSEYE" on the toast (:1919), head never moved. (Pangu 2, Odin 1, Enki 3; r.147 register called it "its own sensor".)
   (a) FALLBACK + SENTENCE (shipped) — cost: an amber appears on a plate the shooter is not looking at, which #1 then asks him to approve or walk to; the fallback reason is on the toast, not yet in the DESIGNATED row.
   (b) REFUSE — the AI marks nothing outside reach, says PUT THE BULLSEYE ON ONE. Cost: AsM SPOT is idle whenever the human looks at grass; the AI stops being a sensor and becomes a confirmer; ASM_SEES_THE_RANGE (:4055) rework.
   DEFAULT: (a) stands; engineer puts `why` in the row (Pangu 3) — reversible in one line.
5. WALL-CLOCK OR FRAME-TIME QUAL TABLES? (prior MoT-10 #5, still open; Odin: `dt=Math.min(.05,…)` :3749 → state.fps ≥ 20 so HAL.PI never engages; Thoth 2's loop-driven QUAL·40 row needs the clock chosen first.)
   (a) WALL-CLOCK + a HIDDEN/PAUSED row — cost: a locked screen or a call LAPSEs exposures on the record; QA rows stepping phys() synthetically rework; rooms need the host EXPOSE row for one clock.
   (b) FRAME TIME (shipped) — cost: a hidden tab pauses a timed table free and unrecorded; "142 S LEFT" is not seconds on a 30 fps phone; QUAL times are not comparable between devices or seats.
   DEFAULT: (b) stays; engineer unclamps state.fps for the HAL gauge only (Odin), which does not touch the table clock.
6. T2/T3 — REACH BEYOND THE BULLSEYE OR REFUSE? r.148 took REFUSE (KEYS_2_3_OBEY_THE_REACH; Aset 2, Enki 5, Odin 2, Sofia 3). At 1× three plates sit inside 48 px of a pip on the 150 R (Thoth), at 3× none.
   (a) REFUSE (shipped) — cost: at 3×+ keys 2/3 and voice "target two" always say NO TARGET; the help line still presents 1/2/3 as equals.
   (b) REACH — next-nearest anywhere on the picture. Cost: key 2 marks a plate 141 px off and swings the head 4.4° (the complaint re-entering by another key, Aset B lane 7).
   DEFAULT: (a) stands; help line rewritten to say "within the ring".
7. IS A PEER'S MARK RE-MARKABLE? r.148 took NEVER RE-SIGNED (:2219: TARGET on a PEER/ASM@ box keeps phase and author; Christo 1, Thor 5 wants HOLD ALREADY_MARKED_BY_PEER as a row).
   (a) NEVER (shipped) — cost: an absent peer's amber sits until a kill, lane change or RESET (all rows); the second seat can only approve it or mark another plate.
   (b) RE-MARKABLE — cost: whichever plate holds the amber keeps taking TARGET (the complaint in a room) and the record rewrites who marked.
   DEFAULT: (a) stands; the HOLD row + "THE OTHER SEAT MARKED IT · PRESS APPROVE OR MARK ANOTHER" is engineer's (Christo 1, Sofia 4).
8. T13 OR THE TRINITY PALETTE? (README §Divergences 1; plan :1365, :1475 — unchanged since r.050; no fleet147 lens raised it.) (a) KEEP T13 — cost: the same Capitol ships in two palettes across the domain plays; vector-law never covers the deck. (b) ADOPT TRINITY — cost: ~30 add() lines, the T13.SI bullseye, the r.013 md5, HI/AI/SI hues re-mapped, saturated hues on the instrument ground at 320 px untested. DEFAULT: T13 stays; nothing on the r.149 path waits on it.
9. ASM CUP FIXTURE — 0.039 OR r.042 AS THE REFERENCE? (asm-cup.ts:14-16 "the operator's to confirm"; plan :1444-1475.) (a) 0.039 — the baseline predates decide/asmTick, so it is a bytes-only fixture, never a behaviour one. ### MoT 11

MoT-11 · PROCESS LESSONS OF 2026-09-23 (r.146→r.148) · read: 12 SYNTH, REVISIONS r.146-148, CLAUDE.md rules · read-only
ONE CLASS BEHIND ALL FIVE: a signal was trusted for what it was NAMED, not for what it WATCHED. Each gate, harness and workflow
proved a property of its own setup and was read as a property of the operator's world.
1. r.147's gate cleared the board, then looked (TARGET_MARKS_THE_PIP: `tgtSlot={}; desig=null` before targetN(1); it drove targetN,
   never the #fTgt button the operator pressed). The fleet reproduced his sentence verbatim on the fix (Athena B, Pangu, Aset, Enki).
   CLASS: the gate reproduced the author's theory (empty board, the helper) not the operator's state (a mark live, the button).
   VIOLATED: FIX THE CLASS #3 (his environment, not your theory) + #5 (review the JOURNEY).
   ADD: **THE GATE IS THE COMPLAINT, REPLAYED.** Before any fix for an operator report, write ONE row that is his sequence verbatim —
   his control (button/key/voice, never the function under it), his prior state (a mark live, the head where the deck parked it),
   his orientation — and watch it go RED on the served build. The fix ships only when THAT row goes green. A row that resets state
   before acting, or drives a helper instead of the surface, is a unit test and is cited as neither gate nor proof.
2. asm-play left the AI OFF since r.133 and wrote "AI never marked" into JSON nobody read (asm-play.mjs:38-39; Enlil A).
   CLASS: a probe that cannot go red — refusal counters, "not exercised", `ok:true` self-exemptions (ASM_MARKED_BY_THE_LOOP:4121).
   VIOLATED: FIX THE CLASS #4 (every invariant gets a gate that FAILS); REPORT THE SHA #4 by analogy (not-run is not a pass).
   ADD: **A PROBE THAT CANNOT GO RED IS NOT EVIDENCE.** Every script cited in REVISIONS.md as proof exits non-zero on the condition it
   names; `refusals`/`skipped`/`not exercised`/`never marked` > 0 is red unless the row prints WHY; zero hits over N shots on any
   seat is red. A JSON field becomes a finding only after something fails on it.
3. The harness scored AI-seat hits from a toast overwritten in 28 ms (Sofia D, Asar 7, Pangu 3): record HIT 6, harness "miss".
   CLASS: a metric taken from a volatile surface instead of the record the document calls the truth; proxy read as source.
   VIOLATED: the ledger thesis (record over glass); FIX THE CLASS #3 ("a mock of the bug you believe in proves only that you believe").
   ADD: **SCORE FROM THE RECORD, ASSERT THE GLASS SEPARATELY.** A harness counts HIT/MISS/REJECT from hashed rows (or the counters they
   feed), never from a toast, class name or caption. The glass gets its own row (result sentence visible ≥ 1400 ms, sampled every
   25 ms). Record ≠ glass is itself the finding, reported as such, never averaged away.
4. Actions "Deploy" concluded `success` for weeks while Build/Deploy/Verify were `skipped` (deploy.yml:76-94; Krishna).
   CLASS: a skip coloured green; a job named for what it did not do; the three states collapsed by the workflow itself.
   VIOLATED: REPORT THE SHA #1 (never collapsed) + #4 (unrun is not a pass) — broken by our own CI, not by a sandbox.
   ADD: **SKIPPED IS A FOURTH STATE, NEVER GREEN.** A job is named for what it proves (Gate, not Deploy) or concludes neutral when it
   skips; `npm run status` prints `actions SKIPPED`, never ✓; "Deploy green" appears in no update unless the deploy steps ran. The
   only word for LIVE is Verify Live's byte compare against the register row; nothing upstream may borrow it.
5. Cloudflare never built 0934636 (no Workers Build, Verify Live red — pushed, gated, never live); the shipping door runs no stamp gate
   and no render-smoke; the ledger names artefact 7a7a2c7, not ship b3d1f63 (Krishna); deck-qa runs only AFTER main (Enlil).
   CLASS: the gates for the last live defect run on the door that never ships; the door that ships runs none; the ship has no record.
   VIOLATED: REPORT THE SHA #3/#4 (LIVE by fetch — held only via a fallback regex; check CF Build history); Release Process #3.
   ADD: **THE DOOR THAT SHIPS RUNS THE GATES, AND THE LEDGER NAMES THE SHIP.** Every gate written for a live defect runs inside the
   command Cloudflare executes (`prebuild`/`postbuild`); deck rows that decide a ship are in `test:ci`, before main. Each release row
   carries `shipped: <sha>` beside the artefact sha, held equal by deck-consistency. A Verify Live red or a missing Workers Build for
   ANY pushed commit is reported as `STALE <sha> · CF build <id|NONE>` in the same message as the push — never found by the next fleet.
SPANNING LINE: **A GATE STATES WHAT IT WATCHED.** Every gate cited in a REVISIONS entry names, in one line: the surface it drove · the
state it started from · what its RED looks like · the door it runs on (test:ci / prebuild / Actions-only). Missing any → "Still open".

### MoT 12

DRONE-2525 · r.148 · THE ONE PAGE FOR THE OPERATOR (MoT 12 of 12 — the 48-agent fold on r.147, 2026-09-23)

1. WHAT THE 48 FOUND — "target approve and fire keeps resetting to 50 m left"
The deck parks your head on the 50 m left plate and your first TARGET press marks it; after that, every TARGET press kept whatever plate was already marked (yours, the other seat's, or the AI's) as long as it sat anywhere inside a wide 35° cone, never looked under your bullseye, and swung your head back onto it — so APPROVE reddened the 50 L under a ring that was on another plate, and FIRE said MISS. With the AI spotter on it was worse: the AI marked the nearest plate in metres the instant a box cleared and physically turned your seat onto it.
Why r.147 did not close it: its "nearest the bullseye" rule ran only on an EMPTY board, and its own test wiped the standing mark before it looked, so the test could not fail; six of twelve lenses reproduced your sentence verbatim on r.147, both orientations, with and without the AI.
What r.148 changed: the eye outranks the mark. A mark is kept only while the bullseye is ON it; otherwise TARGET marks what you are looking at and releases the old mark with a row. TARGET on your own mark keeps it (a red box is never demoted to amber); keys 2/3 and voice obey the same reach as key 1; the AI spots through your bullseye, never moves your head, and waits 1.2 s after a shot so you can read HIT; a FIRE with no box is booked against NONE, not the nearest plate; the other phone can no longer sign your approval or knock your plates down without a red box; the board no longer prints over the strip in portrait.

2. THE TWELVE GRADES AT r.147
Asar C+ · Aset C · Athena D · Christo C+ · Enki C · Enlil B− · Krishna B · Odin C+ · Pangu C · Sofia C · Thor C · Thoth A−.
Convergent verdict: the record and the numbers are honest, but the rule r.147 promised was obeyed by one chooser out of six, and the one control you actually pressed — the TARGET button — had no test at all.

3. PROVEN AT r.148 — AND NOT YET
Proven, headless on the served bytes: nine new rows, 161 total, 160/161 in both orientations (the one red is the sandbox's offline-network row); playable 90/0; two-seat replay 9/9 with one hash on both seats; 100 C marked + bullseye beside the 150 R → TARGET marks the 150 R, head moves 0.63°, old mark released with a row; AI spot on → your head moves 0.000°.
Not proven: a real thumb on the stick holding a ring within a finger-width of an 11 px plate; two physical phones over real Wi-Fi (the room ran on loopback in one browser); a live mic hearing "target two"; the laser-tag rifle's trigger; a phone's true frame rate (the deck's fps gauge cannot read below 20).
The deploy door: r.148 is committed and pushed to main (SHA 10865bd), but the site is UNVERIFIED from this sandbox (403). "Deploy green" in GitHub Actions proves only that the tests ran — with the four secrets unset, the build/deploy/verify steps are skipped and the job still shows green — and Cloudflare, the door that actually ships, runs neither the render smoke nor the timestamp gate.

4. THREE THINGS TO DO NEXT
a) PHONE SCRIPT (portrait, then landscape, PRACTICE): TARGET (50 L amber) → thumb the ring onto the 150 R → TARGET. Pass = "AMBER · 150 M RIGHT" and the head does not jump. APPROVE → FIRE = HIT. Then AsM SPOT on, ring on the 150 R → the AI marks the 150 R and your head does not move. Then two phones: B marks the 250 first; A presses TARGET with its ring on the 50 L → B keeps its mark, A's press marks the 50 L, both hashes MATCH.
b) DECISIONS (yours, not ours): should FIRE refuse and say "your red box is on the 50 M · your ring is not" instead of spending a round on a MISS (r.149 candidate)? Should APPROVE refuse when the target is off the approver's own picture (today it only aims the head)? Should the AI ever be allowed to move a seated human's head before a real gimbal is attached?
c) SECRETS: set NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL, NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY, CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN, CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID in GitHub Actions so Deploy stops meaning "skipped"; in Cloudflare confirm the build command is `npm run build`; then from a networked machine run `cd frontend && npm run status` and open https://exel-ai-polling.explore-096.workers.dev/drone-2525/play.html — expect r.148 (sha256 213a56fc…).

5. SSSES, HONESTLY
SHA 10865bd | committed ✓ | pushed ✓ (main) | cloudflare UNVERIFIED (403) — Stability and Security rose with gates behind them; Scalability and Efficiency print 0 because they were never measured, and Succinctness never counts the deck itself, so the "49 average" is arithmetic over labels, not a score.
